--- Post 1 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Mar 15, 2026
Engagement: 4,425 reactions, 88 comments, 6 reposts (Score: 23,017)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7434656395502784515

12 years ago, I was eating one-dollar tacos to save money as an international student. A few weeks ago, I moved into a 6 bedroom home in North Carolina, which costs $700k.

The sheer size of the backyard and the area of this house is unbelievable. 

My wife, Kaitlyn, literally jumped like a kid doing rope skipping when we walked in for the first time. In that moment, I said to myself, we have come very far from the Kandivali local trains in Mumbai.

But I want to be honest about what lies behind this photo-friendly moment.

It is easy to look at a 700k house and think

- “Lucky”,
- “Must be nice”,
- or “Content creator money”.

I want to remind you that before I moved here, I:

- Said no to trips, gadgets, and comforts because visa, tuition, and savings came first
- Worked full time, built YouTube at night, and hustled as much as I could
- Felt stuck while friends moved to the Bay Area or big tech hubs
- Had A long internal debate about leaving Chico, selling our first home, and starting again on the East Coast

This house is a consequence, a good one at that of those choices.

It is proof that small, consistent decisions add up.

- Choosing the cheaper room so you can avoid debt.
- Choosing to study when it is easier to scroll.
- Choosing to build skills that will still matter 10 years from now.
- Choosing to keep going when the first job, first city or first salary is not what you dreamed of.

If you are an international student or a young engineer who is sharing a flat with three people right now, please hear this:

You do not need to figure out how to buy a 700k house.

You only need to figure out the next right step that moves you forward.

- One better project.
- One stronger internship.
- One more thoughtful application.
- One more skill that increases your leverage.

The big milestones look glamorous on Instagram.
In real life, they are built quietly, one unglamorous choice at a time.

I'm so so grateful for all of you that have helped me, supported me and shown me tons of love in the last six years of my creator journey. I wouldn't have reached here without you. 

I can't thank you enough. 

If you are in your one-dollar taco phase right now, please keep going.
You have no idea what doors your future self is already walking through.

============================================================

--- Post 2 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Mar 15, 2026
Engagement: 1,463 reactions, 89 comments, 1 reposts (Score: 8,207)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7432121922118803456

I just broke up with California, after 11 years together. And this time, we are not getting back.

Yes, a few weeks ago, I said goodbye to California forever, sold my house, packed up my entire last 11 years of life in boxes, and drove over 3,000 miles to the East Coast of the USA.

12 years ago, I came to Chico as a kid from a lower middle income family in Mumbai. Kandivali, local trains, late-night vada pav, a city that never slept.

In America, I landed in this quiet and peaceful town where

- shops shut by 8 pm
- the nearest Indian grocery store is an hour away
- the airport is two hours away
- a direct flight home starts with a three hour drive to San Francisco

First week, I hated it.
By week two, I started to breathe differently.

I fell in love with the trees, the small town where everyone knows everyone, the slow evenings, and the church community that felt like extended family.

I did my master's at Chico State, got my first full-time job, fell in love, got married, bought a home in 2017, and honestly thought, “Maybe this is it. Maybe I will retire here.”

Then life started shifting.
Slowly, the cracks between Chico and me started to appear.

Every year, there was a fire somewhere close.
Every summer came with the same question: Will we be safe?

As I created more content, my travel picked up like crazy.
Twenty to thirty flights a year, each wrapped in four to six hours of driving.

It was exciting, but it was also exhausting.
And somewhere in the middle of all that movement, I started to feel stuck.

Chico gave me everything: my degree, my first job, my marriage, my house…
But it could not give me what I now needed.

Slowly, the weight on my body and mind started to show.
I was travelling the world, but living in one room.

That is when Caitlyn and I had one of the most honest conversations of our lives.

She had grown up in Chico all her life and always dreamt of a bigger city, more cultures, more people.

She looked me in the eye and said, “I want you to thrive. I want us to thrive. Let us not stay small just because it is familiar.”

The hardest part to walk away from was our church community.
Friends who felt like family.
People I could call anytime.

Leaving them hurt.
But staying only out of fear of change hurt more.

So we took the call.

Close the chapter with gratitude.
Keep the memories.
Pack the lessons.

Move toward a life where
- I can rebuild my health
- we can find a community that matches this new season
- and I can grow without driving hours for every connection

Chico will always be the city that made me.
The East Coast is where I find out who I can become next.

============================================================

--- Post 3 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Jan 14, 2026
Engagement: 1,464 reactions, 52 comments, 19 reposts (Score: 7,878)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7409624034922954752

Being a job seeker in this market is basically a long series of painful events.

It is feeling guilty when you rest.
It is feeling guilty when you grind.
It is surviving more than it is living.
It is losing track of weekends and time.
It is hope that keeps getting postponed.
It is wanting to quit and still showing up.
It is watching your savings quietly disappear.
It is reworking your resume for the 50th time.
It is waking up with hope and sleeping with doubt.
It is getting interview calls and still not feeling safe.
It is smiling on calls while anxiety sits in your chest.
It is preparing hard for interviews that never convert.
It is checking your email before you brush your teeth.
It is forcing confidence while your hands are shaking.
It is being strong for everyone and weak only in private.
It is telling yourself “one more try” when you feel empty.
It is celebrating small wins that no one else understands.
It is pretending to be okay when relatives ask for updates.
It is putting your life on pause for a decision you do not control.
It is questioning your skills on good days and your worth on bad ones.
It is seeing “We regret to inform you” more times than your own name.
It is seeing other people get offers and wondering what you are missing.

And yes, if it takes a long while like it did for many of us, it can leave you tired.

Restless.
Anxious.
Even sad.

I recently was talking to someone out there just finished the same long stretch.

Almost the same timeline.

You know what they did? 

They went for a quiet walk and let the last year and months catch up to them.
At some point, you stop carrying the struggle everywhere.
You stop replaying it daily.
You let it rest.

If you can, do something small for someone else this season.

A simple gift.
A small act of kindness.
Something that reminds you that abundance still exists.

Some battles are not meant to be fought loudly.
They are meant to be outlasted.
You do not always have to swing.
Sometimes survival itself is the win.

And for everyone still in the waiting phase
Feeling tired does not mean you are weak.
It’s a phase of life, it will pass and you will come out stronger.

============================================================

--- Post 4 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Jan 14, 2026
Engagement: 1,321 reactions, 50 comments, 26 reposts (Score: 7,157)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7410711070035587073

If you are making $80k a year, you will be jealous of someone making $120k.

If you are at a small company, you will be jealous of someone at FAANG.

If you are at FAANG, you will be jealous of someone at the next hot startup.

Pick your jealousy.
There is an unlimited supply.

And it guarantees only one thing.
Permanent dissatisfaction.

It is the same with job hunting.

You might be consistent.
Someone else lands faster.

You might be learning deeply.
Someone else cracks a shortcut.

You might be building long-term skill.
Someone else gets a lucky break.

That does not make you lesser.

The only comparison that makes sense
is who you were six months ago.

If that version is growing,
you are already winning.

Growth is not a leaderboard.
Careers are not a race clock.
And peace never comes from comparison.

It only comes when you can look at your past self and say,
“I am stronger than I was.”

If you do not learn this,
every new milestone will only teach your ego a more expensive way to stay unhappy.

============================================================

--- Post 5 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Jan 14, 2026
Engagement: 1,036 reactions, 43 comments, 3 reposts (Score: 5,616)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7408174364816269312

In 2011, I failed the first semester of my diploma and felt completely hopeless about my future.

In 2025, that same person is living a life he could not have even imagined back then.
Today, I am a creator. A husband. A mentor. A Product Manager.

My garage today in the USA is the size of the house I spent my childhood in, and I look at it as a reminder that we all have the power to will our dreams into existence. 

I was born in a one-room chawl in Mumbai. Naturally, things were tough. 
My dad worked factory jobs and did sales to keep our kitchen running.
My mom managed the household and stretched everything to make ends meet.

But even then, many months fell short.

By 2012, I had finished engineering from DJ Sanghvi, but inside, I felt lost and unsure.

By 2014, I took the biggest emotional and financial bet of my life.
I borrowed money from friends and family and came to the U.S. for my MS.

No one in my family had done what I was thinking would work for me.

In 2016, I graduated with a Master’s in Computer Science from Chico State.
Three job offers came together.
And for the first time, life felt steady.

In 2017, I slowly paid off my education loan.
Bought my first car.
Bought my first home.
My survival had turned into stability.

In 2018, I started posting online without any expectations.
My inbox slowly filled with messages from students saying,
“I wish I knew this earlier.”

And now, years later, I balance a demanding full-time role and a creator life.
Thousands of students from different parts of the world now learn with us.
I talk about careers, visas, fear, confidence, and purpose.

And yet, even today, when I park my car in my garage,
my mind still goes back to that one-room home in Mumbai.
But with gratitude.

This did not happen in one day. It happened because I kept showing up when results were invisible.

When motivation was low.
When timelines made no sense.

If my life has taught me one thing, it is this.

Nothing big changes in a day. 
Everything big changes when you stay consistent for years.

So if you are an international student.
Or someone sitting in a small room with big dreams.
Or someone who feels behind right now.

Let this be your reminder.

Think in years.
Not in weeks.
Not in the one bad month you are stuck in.

One year of steady effort can take you further than a decade of wishing.

Good luck with your dreams!

============================================================

If you are international student then this might be something your are going through

Burnout isn’t about studying too hard.

It’s about trying to prove yourself to people who never understood your journey.

↳You’re the one who actually cares.
↳Flying across the world to chase your dream.
↳Working two jobs just to pay rent.
↳Sending applications to companies that don’t even look at internationals.

And slowly, you start to feel it:

The exhaustion that sleep can’t fix
The loneliness that FaceTime calls don’t erase
The pressure of visas, deadlines, and “settling down” questions

You’re not weak.

You’re human.

Here’s how international students protect their energy:

Notice the pressure sources
→ Who are you really trying to impress — yourself or others?

Set boundaries with expectations
→ You don’t need to explain your struggle to everyone.

Stop overgiving to the wrong places
→ Not every company, classmate, or relative deserves your all.

Track your progress
→ Celebrate every small win — they add up.

Find your allies
→ Lean on the few who truly understand what you’re carrying.

Some people will never understand your path.

And that’s okay.

Your job isn’t to make them understand.
It’s to protect your ability to keep going.
💬 Comment the one line you wish people understood about your journey.
 🔁 And if this spoke to you — share it forward. Someone you know needs it.

Author: priteshjagani

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7423057979437940736

A job seeker in my community wasted 6 weeks applying to the same jobs as everyone else. He spammed easy apply, and every job he would see on LinkedIn looked like a ghost job been there for ages. 

The first time I got to know about him, I gave him a way to find 20+ job listings daily instead of the daily ghost jobs that 1000s of candidates see.

Btw, I run a community of 5100+ jobseekers, my Job Hunting Accelerator, where I've regularly given this advice to so many people. 

We've got modules for networking, job hunting strategy, and have tons of guest sessions as well. Check it out here: https://lnkd.in/gt5YBAbr

You see, companies post jobs on their career pages before LinkedIn. 
Sometimes hours before. Sometimes days.

And almost nobody is looking there.

Last Tuesday at 9 AM, I found a Product Manager role at a Series B startup. Posted 3 hours earlier. 2 applicants when I saw it. 

That same role hit LinkedIn Thursday afternoon. 
847 applicants by Friday.

I could’ve landed the interview, and those 845 other people would still be waiting.

Here's what I figured out:

Most companies use the same handful of job platforms. 
Workday, Greenhouse, Lever, iCIMS, SmartRecruiters.

Google knows exactly where they are. Here’s a search that finds every new posting across these platforms in seconds.

The base search:

("your job title") (site:myworkdayjobs.com OR site:greenhouse.io OR site:lever.co OR site:smartrecruiters.com OR site:icims.com)

That's it. Replace "your job title" with whatever you're looking for.

The game changer is sorting by recency. After you run the search, look at the URL in your browser. Add this to the very end:

&tbs=qdr:d

Now you only see jobs posted in the last day.

Do this every morning. Takes 20 minutes.

Find 10-15 fresh postings. 
Scan them fast. 

Apply to the best 3-4 before anyone else even knows they exist.

His results after switching:

Week 1: 12 applications, 3 responses
Week 2: 15 applications, 5 responses
Week 3: 18 applications, 6 responses
Week 4: 2 offers

LinkedIn posts take time to populate. By the time you see them, hundreds of people already applied. Hiring managers are buried. Your resume goes to the bottom of a pile that never gets fully reviewed.

Direct ATS applications land when the inbox is empty. 
You're applicant number 4, not applicant 432.

What role are you searching for?
Use this custom GPT to help you out: https://lnkd.in/gsQhGDeN

--- Post 10 ---
Author: priteshjagani

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7416526478554681344

As an international student in the US, you will often feel like you are behind in life.
You might be 24, 29, or 32.

Some friends already have H1Bs.
Some are buying cars and houses.
Some are getting promoted while you are still trying to clear your first job search or visa step.

It was simple back home.
Score well in exams, and everyone knows you are doing fine.

Here, it feels like there is a new scoreboard every week.

LinkedIn, Instagram, and WhatsApp groups. Always someone posting a bigger salary, a better company, a faster visa, a nicer apartment.

Slowly, two things happen.
You feel smaller, and you start seeing people as competition instead of community.

That is where many people go wrong. 

They stop sharing job links.
They hide how they got referrals.
They gatekeep lawyers, tax tips, and visa updates.
They start resenting the friend who got lucky in the lottery or landed a big offer.

But…

Jealousy will not change your status.
Gatekeeping will not bring you one extra interview.
Bitterness will not pay your rent or build your credit score.

But your actions will always say everything about you.
Their actions will always say everything about them.

So choose your side carefully.

Choose to:

– Share information about visa rules and deadlines when you learn something useful
– Help your friends review their resumes before big applications
– Tell people which tax tools, credit card tips and budgeting tricks are actually working for you
– Celebrate their wins even when your own life feels slow
– Build deep relationships instead of sitting alone with Google and anxiety

Because staying in the US is not just about one job offer or one lottery result.

It is about:

– Knowing your immigration timelines and planning early
– Building a basic personal finance system so you are not one paycheck away from panic
– Having friends who would show up if your visa, health, or job went wrong
– Becoming the kind of person others want to refer to, vouch for, and live with

Kindness is not weakness.
In this country, it is a long-term strategy.

I still hang out with my ex-boss from my Chico days. We play poker, we chat, we hang out, and it’s been over 10 years, one of the best relationships I’ve cultivated in life. 

Think big. Don’t let jealousy narrow your vision. 

–
P.S: Every week, I share insights for international students and job seekers on my newsletter. Join 45,000+ readers who are turning their U.S. journey into a success story worth telling. Link: https://lnkd.in/dKkBVpxd

Author: priteshjagani

My very first year in the USA as an international student went exactly like this:
- 45,000 dollars in an education loan
- Rejected by 750 companies
- Constant fear that my pivot from CS to PM would fail
- Terrified, I would not be able to pay the loan back

I remember lying awake at night doing mental math.
- If I have no internship.
- If I did not get an H1B.
- If my visa ran out.

Every path in my head ended in one word: Failure.
Back then, "success" looked very simple to me.

Get a job.
Pay off the loan.
That was the entire dream.

Fast forward to my 11th year in the USA, and I still have moments where I look around and think, "How is this my life?"

It has evolved in so many more beautiful ways than I can count.

None of this happened in one lucky year.
It happened in small, unglamorous chapters.

You may look at your life right now, and it can feel like you are already late.
You see people on LinkedIn who "made it" in 3 years.

You see friends switching jobs while you are still chasing your first break.
Here is what I wish someone had told me in that first year:

- Your rejection count is high, but it is not a verdict on your potential.
- Your pivot feels risky, but it might be the reason your career compounds so fast later.

You are allowed to hold to be scared and still keep moving at the same time.
Today, when I look back after more than a decade, this is what I know for sure:

You have to think in years, not weeks.
You have to think in the bigger picture.

You have already survived 100 percent of your hardest days.
There will be more hard days.
There will be more doubt.

There will be another version of you who feels like a beginner again at the next level. But you are capable. You are deserving.

You are strong enough not only to build your dream life,
but to hold it, grow it, and share it with others long after you get there.

If you are in your "first year" right now in any area of life, remember: This chapter is not the whole book.

Stay locked in. Give yourself a decade. You will be shocked at what becomes possible when you do not quit on yourself.

============================================================

Most people use AI.
Few people become AI-powered.

Last year, I was juggling 12 tools a day - docs, slides, edits, emails and still burning out.
I thought working harder was the solution.
Turns out, working smarter with AI was the real game-changer.

The moment I automated half my workflow with AI tools, everything shifted - faster results, more creativity, zero burnout.

1. Create Like a Pro, Not a Robot
Use tools like Jasper, Gamma, and Kittl to turn ideas into polished content that looks and reads like it was built by a team, not one tired human.

2. Automate the Boring Stuff
Make and Fireflies handle the repetitive chaos. AI isn’t just about creativity, it’s about reclaiming your time.

3. Learn & Grow at Light Speed
Platforms like StudyPal and Perplexity make learning frictionless. No fluff. Just the insights that actually matter.

4. Collaborate Smarter, Not Harder
With Taskade and Chatbase, teams can brainstorm, plan, and support customers - all without drowning in meetings or tickets.

5. Build an AI-Driven Career Stack
From Kickresume to Akkio, every part of your professional journey can now be powered by AI, if you know where to look.

The future doesn’t belong to those who fear AI.
It belongs to those who partner with it.

You don’t get replaced by AI, you get replaced by someone who knows how to use it.

And if you want to turn recruiter visibility into real job offers, that’s exactly what we teach inside the Job Hunting Accelerator,
where Big Tech recruiters train international students to win in today’s market: https://lnkd.in/gxt5X9BC

--- Post 18 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Dec 15, 2025
Engagement: 459 reactions, 74 comments, 19 reposts (Score: 3,073)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7404188104821100545

A job seeker in my community wasted 6 weeks applying to the same jobs as everyone else. 
He spammed easy apply, and every job he would see on LinkedIn looked like a ghost job been there for ages. 

The first time I got to know about him, I gave him a way to find 20+ job listings daily instead of the daily ghost jobs that 1000s of candidates see.

It is something obvious hiding in plain sight for everyone. 

Companies post jobs on their career pages before LinkedIn. 
Sometimes hours before. Sometimes days.

And almost nobody is looking there.

Last Tuesday at 9 AM, I found a Product Manager role at a Series B startup. Posted 3 hours earlier. 2 applicants when I saw it. 

That same role hit LinkedIn Thursday afternoon. 
847 applicants by Friday.

I could’ve landed the interview, and those 845 other people would still be waiting.

Here's what I figured out:

Most companies use the same handful of job platforms. 
Workday, Greenhouse, Lever, iCIMS, SmartRecruiters.

Google knows exactly where they are. Here’s a search that finds every new posting across these platforms in seconds.

The base search:

("your job title") (site:myworkdayjobs.com OR site:greenhouse.io OR site:lever.co OR site:smartrecruiters.com OR site:icims.com)

That's it. Replace "your job title" with whatever you're looking for.

The game changer is sorting by recency. After you run the search, look at the URL in your browser. Add this to the very end:

&tbs=qdr:d

Now you only see jobs posted in the last day.

Do this every morning. Takes 20 minutes.

Find 10-15 fresh postings. 
Scan them fast. 

Apply to the best 3-4 before anyone else even knows they exist.

His results after switching:

Week 1: 12 applications, 3 responses
Week 2: 15 applications, 5 responses
Week 3: 18 applications, 6 responses
Week 4: 2 offers

LinkedIn posts take time to populate. By the time you see them, hundreds of people already applied. Hiring managers are buried. Your resume goes to the bottom of a pile that never gets fully reviewed.

Direct ATS applications land when the inbox is empty. 
You're applicant number 4, not applicant 432.

What role are you searching for?
Comment “Custom Query” and I’ll give you a custom GPT chat link that you can use to do it in seconds..

P.S: Every week, I share free insights like this in my job hunting community with over 4100+ people. If you want to get hired in the next 3 months, join Yudi J academy: https://lnkd.in/gWWZJDEf

AI Agents are no longer experimental, they’re reshaping every tech career, from Product Managers to Data Scientists. They automate repetitive tasks, boost decision-making, and free teams to focus on strategy and innovation.

From QA testing to data cleaning, cybersecurity monitoring to DevOps automation, AI Agents are becoming the invisible teammates every professional needs.

Here are some real-world use cases across tech roles:
1. Product Managers → Automate roadmap insights, competitor analysis, and release notes.
2. Software Engineers → Speed up testing, bug detection, and code generation.
3. Data Scientists → Optimize model training, experiment tracking, and bias detection.
4. Cybersecurity Specialists → Run phishing filters, anomaly detection, and auto-remediation.
5. DevOps Engineers → Automate deployments, error tracking, and resource scaling.
6. UI/UX Designers → Generate wireframe suggestions and usability reports.
7. AI/ML Engineers → Handle model deployment, drift detection, and explainable AI.
8. Data Engineers → Manage ETL automation, schema mapping, and data validation.

The takeaway? AI Agents are not replacing jobs - they’re evolving roles and helping tech professionals level up faster than ever.

My life in the USA as an international student could have been completely different and I would not have struggled for 2 to 3 brutal years if I had understood these things when I first landed in 2014.

1. I realized loneliness here is not just “missing home”
 ↳ It is having no one who truly believes in you when your plans start collapsing.

2. I stopped letting insecure people decide my career direction
↳ Everyone told me switching from CS to Product was a stupid risk. I listened to my gut anyway.

3. I found one mentor who saw what others could not
↳ One person told me, “Use your tech plus people skills.” That single sentence changed my life.

4. I understood that your circle decides your ceiling
 ↳ When I worked with high-net-worth professionals, my entire relationship with money shifted forever.

5. I learned that skills mean nothing without communication
 ↳ The average candidate with confidence and clarity beats the skilled one who cannot articulate value.

6. I should’ve started job preparation before even landing in the US
↳ The ones who win give themselves a six-month head start. The rest start only when panic hits.

7. I stopped waiting to feel confident before networking
↳ I treated job hunting like dating. You go where the right people already are.

8. I became immune to rejection
↳ When the upside can change your life, rejection stops feeling personal.

9. I learned to invest in S&P Me before S&P 500
↳ Conferences. Tools. Learning. Education is not an expense. It is leverage on your future.

Now this is not easy.

Most international students stay stuck because they

– Wait to “settle first”
– Avoid spending on learning
– Stay in the wrong circles
– Hide during job search
– Delay preparation until fear takes over

And then they wonder why progress feels impossible.

If you are an international student reading this
And you relate to even one of these shifts
Your life can change faster than you think.

And it won’t be because the market becomes easy.
It will be because you finally become intentional and grip your career direction with both hands. 

–

--- Post 24 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Feb 13, 2026
Engagement: 401 reactions, 43 comments, 4 reposts (Score: 2,443)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7427095106702462976

6 years ago, this was a stranger. Today, he’s one of my closest friends.
And he taught me one of the MOST important lessons on kindness 👇

A few months ago, I was going through a tough moment.

While looking through comments to respond to on a few Instagram videos, I noticed 2-3 cruel ones.

I’m someone who feels things deeply, so it got to me more than I expected.
I didn’t know who to call.

Not everyone understands what it feels like to be criticized by strangers across the world through a cold, impersonal screen.

But I knew one person would, so I called him.

—

Yudi (Pritesh Jagani) and I met more than six years ago through an online conference organized by Robert Adams ☕️. Back then, he was just another creator in my orbit, someone doing good work for international students.

In 2021, we met once to record a podcast together.

And for most of the last six years, that’s how it stayed.

Our paths crossed occasionally, but our lives didn’t overlap much.

Until the last year.

Somehow, we ended up on dozens of long calls last year - and I got to know him beyond his work, and vice versa.

That day, when I called him feeling distraught, he listened quietly for the first half of the call.

“How do you deal with comments like this?” I asked eventually.

He paused and said something that changed how I see the internet:

“When I see a critical or hateful comment,” he said, “I first see if there’s any truth in it. Sometimes, there is. Most of the times though, it’s just hate. That’s when my brain immediately switches to wanting to give that person an extra dose of kindness. Clearly, something is off. Most of the time, it’s not about you. They might just be having a bad day or going through something we don’t know. So I respond with kindness. And strangely, a lot of the time, they respond with kindness too.”

The thing is… I never scroll social media.
I don’t have the apps on my phone.
I only check it on my laptop, and even then, block the newsfeed and check people’s profiles directly.

But the few times I’ve really seen what endless scrolling does to people - it’s overwhelming.
Triggering.
And it can bring out the worst in us.

After what Yudi said, I realized that when I see criticism,
Not only should I NOT fight back or get defensive,
But also respond with an extra dose of kindness.

Now, when I see a critical comment, there’s still a momentary urge to defend myself or say something witty.
But it passes quickly.
In its place, there’s a reminder that there is a whole human being on the other side of the screen - someone with dreams, hopes, and their own version of suffering.

Responding with kindness also gave me something unexpected: Peace.

Deeply grateful to Yudi for changing my perspective 💚

—

🌿 Re-post if this resonated. 👉 I share weekly stories of impactful people, companies, and art. Join 37,000+ here: https://lnkd.in/gnrA8GJZ

============================================================

Merry Christmas.
And thank you for being part of this community 🤍

Hope did not come when the world was perfect.
It came when it was messy.
And that is still true today.

As this year comes to an end, I find myself feeling deeply grateful.

This year stretched me in ways I did not expect.
Personally. Professionally. Emotionally.

There were moments of clarity.
There were moments of doubt.
There were seasons where growth felt quiet, slow, and invisible.

But looking back, I can see it clearly now.
I grew.
Not just in outcomes, but in perspective, patience, and purpose.

I am especially grateful for this community.
For the conversations.
For the messages that said, “This helped me,” when I did not even realize how much I needed to hear that too.
For trusting me with your stories, struggles, and dreams.

I pray that as we move into the new year, you experience hope even if life feels messy right now.

That you recognize how far you have come, even if you are not exactly where you want to be yet.

And that you give yourself grace for the journey.

“Applying Endlessly" to every job you see online won't get you a Job

Landing great roles follow a structured system - learning the right skills, building proof of work, networking intentionally, and using AI to speed up every step.

Here’s a step-by-step ladder showing the exact path to go from zero clarity → real skills → strong projects → confident interviews → a job in 2026.

Follow the ladder one step at a time, stay consistent, and let AI become your accelerator - not your replacement.

Here’s the breakdown : 

1. Start With Awareness – Understand 2026 job trends and what companies actually want.
2. Align With Goals – Pick your direction, map your skills, and set learning goals.
3. Research What Companies Hire For – Study job listings, patterns, and real expectations.
4. Learn the Skills (Smartly) – Follow a structured roadmap instead of random tutorials.
5. Build Projects That Prove Your Skills – Solve real problems and show end-to-end work.
6. Create a Strong Portfolio – Upload polished projects with clear documentation.
7. Write a Job-Winning Resume – Keep it clean, quantified, and ATS-friendly.
8. Apply Strategically (Not Randomly) – Target companies and track applications.
9. Prepare for Interviews – Practice behavioral + technical challenges intentionally.
10. Build Visibility & Network – Share learnings, engage publicly, connect with recruiters.
11. Boost Consistency – Review weekly, fix weaknesses, build discipline.
12. Avoid Pitfalls – Don’t overuse AI or chase 10 skills at once.
13. Recharge & Reflect – Prevent burnout and adjust your strategy.
14. Power Your Career With AI – Use AI for learning faster, interview prep, workflow, and project building.
15. Publish & Share – Teach what you learn, attract opportunities, and stand out.

If you follow this ladder with patience, clarity, and consistency, getting a job in 2026 becomes a predictable process - not a guessing game.

Start small, scale your efforts with AI, share your progress, and trust the journey. Your next opportunity is built one step at a time.

The 7 Hills Every Job Seeker Must Climb to Become Irreplaceable in 2026

AI won’t replace you, but someone who knows how to use AI absolutely will.
The future belongs to job seekers who master these seven core areas.

1️⃣ Awareness & Market Understanding
Know how industries are shifting and what companies will value next so you can stay ahead of the curve.

2️⃣ Skill Mastery & Deep Learning
Go beyond theory and develop real technical depth through consistent practice and project-based learning.

3️⃣ Proof-of-Work Portfolio
Show what you can do through strong, practical projects that demonstrate real impact and problem-solving ability.

4️⃣ Communication & Personal Branding
Build a visible, credible identity online by telling your story clearly and sharing your expertise confidently.

5️⃣ Resume, Applications & Positioning
Apply strategically with tailored resumes and positioning that make your fit obvious within seconds.

6️⃣ Interview Performance & Confidence
Improve your clarity, presence, and confidence through mock interviews and repeatable storytelling frameworks.

7️⃣ Adaptability, AI Fluency & Consistency
Embrace new tools quickly, use AI to boost your productivity, and deliver consistent results over time.

Climb these seven hills, and no AI will ever replace you.
You’ll be the one using AI to lead, build, and thrive.

--- Post 34 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Mar 31, 2026
Engagement: 357 reactions, 9 comments (Score: 1,875)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7443347208953028608

The Bay Area is great until you realize you’re paying to be stressed, rushed, and permanently “one more year away” from peace. 

For 11 years, I lived in Chico, and this year we packed up our lives and moved to Raleigh, and a lot of people asked me why?

Kaitlyn and I didn’t decide our way into this move randomly. We wrote down our criteria and thought about it for a long time. 

My non-negotiables were simple:

1. An airport that’s actually close
2. An Indian community
3. Church community
4. Lower taxes
5. Tech presence, even if I’m remote
6. Mountains and water

Once we had the list, we did what everyone does now. We used AI to generate options, then we filtered them with our criteria. 

The Bay Area and San Diego were immediately out. Cost and taxes. Jersey City was in the mix. Seattle was in the mix. Arlington, Virginia, and Florida were in the mix.

Then Raleigh and Charlotte kept showing up. What changed everything for us was visiting.

You see, Raleigh isn’t just Raleigh. The bigger story is RTP, the Research Triangle Park.

Raleigh.
UNC Chapel Hill.
Duke in Durham.

That triangle creates a good ecosystem. Tons of tech companies. Big Indian community. Great universities, which also matter for my work and audience.

We visited for 10 days and did one thing every night: tried to find red flags. We couldn’t.

Then we found our fit in neighborhoods like Cary and Morrisville.

– Morrisville is 10 minutes from the airport.
– Raleigh is 15 minutes away.
– DC is 3 hours by road.
– Most East Coast cities are 1-hour flights.
– Mountains and beaches are within driving range.

And yes, the Indian food, movies, and community are all there.

For others, I also want to mention you’re under 30 and still building your career: bigger cities still have unmatched opportunities. If you’re early in your journey, places like the Bay Area, Seattle, Austin, and NYC can still be the right move.

For me, at this stage, I wanted a place that checked every box for family, community, travel, and long-term peace. That’s why I chose Raleigh. Or more accurately, Morrisville.

============================================================

--- Post 35 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Oct 16, 2025
Engagement: 297 reactions, 22 comments, 49 reposts (Score: 1,803)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7377373438002245632

AI can be a superpower for a Product Manager.

From creating roadmap & strategy to customer support & reporting, AI tools are helping how PMs work, decide, and deliver.

But here’s the real question: Do you need paid tools, or can free AI-powered alternatives do the job?

This comparison breaks it down for you:

- Paid Tools like Productboard, SimilarWeb Pro, Qualtrics, Amplitude, and Intercom bring enterprise-grade features, integrations, and advanced analytics which are perfect for scaling teams.

- Free Tools like Notion AI, Ubersuggest, Trello AI, PostHog, and Canva AI still give you powerful functionality, making them ideal for startups, solopreneurs, or PMs experimenting with AI.

From task automation with Zapier AI vs Make to presentation design with Beautifulai vs Canva AI, there’s a balance between affordability and functionality depending on your stage and needs.

Conclusion :  Paid tools unlock advanced capabilities, but free AI tools are more than enough to get started and stay competitive.

Which side are you on - Paid or Free AI tools for Product Management? Let Me Know In Comments !

P.S. Follow Pritesh Jagani for content like project managent and getting a job in tech field. 

Repost to help your network.

Author: priteshjagani

One thing I wish I could tell every incoming international student in 2026 before you spend $100,000 on your MS in the USA and are left with nothing but debt.

An incoming student in India got admits from six universities: Tufts, NC State, Texas A&M, Johns Hopkins, USC, and Northeastern.

On our livestream, we told him: Do not consider USC. Do not consider Northeastern. And this is exactly why... 

(Context: This post is based on my Conversation from the livestream on my YT Channel, you can check it out as well)

Two of those admits have brand recognition. USC in Los Angeles. Northeastern in Boston. 

Both will cost you upwards of $100,000. 
Both sound impressive when you tell your relatives.

Neither will give you what you actually need.

If finances are not a constraint, Johns Hopkins.
If finances matter even a little, Texas A&M.

Johns Hopkins: Top engineering management outcomes. Strong DMV network. High price, but justified.

Texas A&M: Half the cost. Same ROI. Strong Texas connections. Competitive cohort.

Why Not USC?
- Tuition: $48,000+ per year
- LA living: $2,000-3,000/month
- Bay Area prices without Bay Area outcomes
- Better MEM programs exist for less

Why Not Northeastern?
- Cohort: 1,000+ students in some programs
- You're a number, not a name
- TA/RA opportunities? Nearly impossible
- Boston rent doesn't justify the outcomes

The Others:
Tufts: Strong ROI. If you got $30K-40K scholarship, it's in serious play.
NC State: Decent for construction/industrial focus. Not top tier but solid.

What You Should Actually Do:

1. Look at the money. Even a $20,000 gap between Hopkins and Texas A&M? Pick Texas A&M.

2. Ignore the "sounds good to parents" factor. When you're $80,000 in debt with no job, prestige doesn't pay loans.

3. Check cohort size. Smaller = professors know you = better TA positions = better recommendations.

4. Think domain. Healthcare → Hopkins. Manufacturing → Texas A&M. General PM → pick affordable to you.

Bottom Line: Texas A&M at $60,000 total > USC at $150,000 total for the same outcome.

P.S. I send a weekly newsletter on immigration and job-hunting. Join 35,000+ readers: https://lnkd.in/eFxuSc38

Disclaimer: I'm not an admissions counselor nor have I anything against any university . This is based on our team's experience at Gradvine with thousands of students. Please do your own research before you make a decision.

When I was in college, I used to spend hours switching between tabs, juggling notes, and racing deadlines.

Research felt endless. Writing essays was painful. And group projects? Pure chaos.

But students today have something we didn’t - AI tools that make studying smarter, not harder.

Here are the Top 30 AI Tools Every Student Should Know in 2025:

Research & Learning
• ChatGPT – Simplify complex topics and generate explanations.
• Elicit AI – Find academic papers and summarize them.
• ChatPDF – Chat with your PDFs instead of reading 40 pages.
• Consensus AI – Get evidence-based answers from scientific papers.
• Perplexity AI – Ask questions and get cited, well-researched results.

Writing & Essays
• Grammarly – Fix grammar, tone, and clarity instantly.
• Jasper AI – Create essays and articles with natural flow.
• Copyai – Generate ideas and write drafts faster.
• QuillBot – Paraphrase or summarize long text with ease.
• Wordtune – Rewrite sentences for smoother academic writing.

Productivity & Organization
• Notion AI – Manage notes, projects, and tasks in one place.
• Todoist AI – Organize study plans and to-do lists effortlessly.
• Motion – Automate your calendar and schedule study sessions.
• Readwise – Save and recall key highlights from research.
• Rowscom – Automate spreadsheets and simplify analysis.

Presentations & Creativity
• SlidesAI – Turn notes into professional slides in minutes.
• Gamma App – Design modern presentations without PowerPoint.
• Canva Magic Write – Create visuals and text with AI help.
• Framer AI – Build websites for projects visually and quickly.
• Beautifulai – Design aesthetic presentations automatically.

Study Assistance & Exam Prep
• Quizlet AI – Create flashcards and quizzes automatically.
• Socratic by Google – Scan questions and get instant explanations.
• TutorAI – Learn any topic interactively with AI tutors.
• Explainpaper – Break down complex research papers.
• Upword – Summarize long documents into clear notes.

Communication & Collaboration
• Otterai – Record and transcribe lectures or discussions.
• Firefliesai – Summarize meetings or group calls.
• Descript – Edit video/audio projects like a document.
• HeyGen – Create AI videos for projects or assignments.
• Claude AI – Collaborate with a context-aware writing assistant.

AI is not replacing learning - it’s enhancing how we learn.

The students who use these tools wisely will graduate with more time, more skills, and less stress.

The U.S. has quietly lost nearly half of its international students.

Outside of COVID, this is the largest enrollment crash we’ve ever seen.

• Overall drop: 40–50%
• China: down 12%
• India: down 44%
• These two countries once made up almost half of all international students

Why is this happening?

– Harder student visa approvals
– Lack of appointment availability
– Travel restrictions
– Growing immigration uncertainty

And here’s what many people miss:

Over 80% of international students are in STEM.
They fund universities through higher tuition.
They contribute to research in AI, medicine, engineering, and emerging technologies.

If these students choose Ireland, Germany, Canada, or Australia instead,
The U.S. doesn’t just lose tuition revenue.

It risks losing long-term innovation leadership.

Is America unintentionally pushing away the very talent that drives its economy?

Would love to hear your thoughts.

--- Post 46 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Feb 13, 2026
Engagement: 310 reactions, 7 comments, 4 reposts (Score: 1,628)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7427039898387345408

Everyone's posting "Texas banned H-1Bs," and I'm here to tell you: if you work for a private company in Texas, absolutely nothing changed for you.

My DMs exploded after the news:
- I work in Austin, am I screwed?
- Does this affect my H-1B transfer?
- Should I move states?"

Let me clear this up.

Btw, Every Wednesday, I chat with an immigration lawyer from Manifest Law on my YT live. I'll be having another livestream this Wednesday as well, do join :)

➤ What Governor Abbott Actually Did

He paused H-1B sponsorships for:
- State agencies (government jobs)
- Public universities (UT Austin, Texas A&M, etc.)

If you work for a private employer in Texas, your H-1B process is completely unaffected.

➤ Who This Actually Impacts
Let's say you're a researcher at UT Austin on STEM OPT and they were planning to sponsor your H-1B this March. That's now paused for one year (until May 2027).

Or you work for a Texas state agency and were counting on H-1B sponsorship. Also paused.

But if you're a software engineer at a private company in Austin? Your H-1B transfer, extension, or new filing proceeds exactly as it would have last week.

➤ Can Other States Do This?

Yes, technically. Governors have authority over state-funded entities. They can tell public universities and state agencies what to do with their hiring and visa sponsorship.

But they cannot tell private companies how to run their businesses. So could Florida do something similar for state universities? Possibly.

Could Florida ban all H-1Bs across the entire state for private employers? No. That's not within a governor's power.

➤ What You Should Actually Worry About

If you work for a Texas public university:

1. This pause might not end in one year. Governor Abbott wants agencies to "defend" why they need H-1B workers. The pause could extend.

2. Start exploring alternatives now:
 - Can you find a private employer willing to sponsor?
 - Can you build your own pathway (O-1, EB-1A)?
 - Do you have time on OPT to ride this out?
3. If your extension is already filed and pending, you should be fine. The pause is for new filings.

➤ The Bigger Picture
Texas has the highest number of H-1B sponsoring entities in the country. This news made waves because of that stat.

But the actual number of people affected by this pause? It's a fraction of what the headlines suggested.

If you're at a state university, take this seriously and have a backup plan.
If you're at a private company, breathe. You're fine.

Disclaimer: I am not an attorney. This post is for educational purposes only and is based on my public conversation with an immigration lawyer. Your case may be very different. Always speak with a qualified immigration professional who can review your specific situation.

How To Land a High-Paying Job Before 2026

AI tools, global hiring, and skill-based recruitment are changing how careers grow.

It’s no more about sending resumes - it’s about standing out, staying consistent, and playing the long game.

Here’s how to stay ahead and secure your next high-paying role before 2025:

1. Role-Specific Resume
Recruiters spot generic resumes instantly.
Tailor your resume for each role using AI Resume Builders for precision.

2. Customize Templates
Have a library of cover letters and DM templates ready.
Personalize 2–3 lines for each role, it saves time while showing effort.

3. Celebrate Small Wins
Even a recruiter’s reply is progress.
Every small step is proof you’re moving closer to your goal.

4. Boost Your LinkedIn Profile
Share updates, stay active, and keep your headline clear.
Use free AI tools to optimize your LinkedIn for visibility.

5. Set Realistic Targets
You don’t need to apply to 100 jobs a day.
Focus on 5–10 high-quality applications that match your skills.

6. Grow While Applying
If callbacks are slow, add short courses or side projects.
Learning while applying shows initiative, not desperation.

7. Mental Health Matters
Rejections happen - it’s not you, it’s the market.
Take breaks, set boundaries, and lean on your support system.

8. Job Hunt in Blocks
Block 1–2 hours daily for job applications, networking, and follow-ups.
Consistency beats intensity.

9. Track Everything
Use AI Job Trackers or spreadsheets to stay organized.
Tracking = clarity = faster progress.

10. Follow Up Smartly
Don’t just apply and wait.
Message recruiters 7–10 days after applying, smart candidates stay top of mind.

Remember: Every rejection is redirection - every small step counts.
Stay focused, stay consistent, and the right opportunity will find you.

Don’t just apply and wait.
Message recruiters 7–10 days after applying, smart candidates stay top of mind.

Remember: Every rejection is redirection - every small step counts.
Stay focused, stay consistent, and the right opportunity will find you.

Stop using the wrong AI for your job search

Most job seekers are leaving opportunities on the table because they're using one AI tool for everything.

The truth? Each AI model has unique strengths. ChatGPT excels at personalized content. Perplexity delivers research-backed insights. Grok catches real-time trends. Gemini powers through documentation.

Using the wrong tool is like bringing a hammer to every job - sometimes you need a screwdriver.

Here's the framework that's helping smart candidates land offers faster:

- ChatGPT = Your Personal Career Assistant → Tailored resumes for each role → Cover letters that actually get read → Mock interview prep that feels real → Emails to recruiters that get responses

- Perplexity = Your Research Engine → Real salary data for negotiations → Expert insights on breaking into roles → Company research with actual sources → Skills analysis of top candidates

- Grok = Your Trend Detector → Emerging roles before they explode → What skills are trending in your industry → Hidden hiring signals on social media → Early adopter job search strategies

- Gemini = Your Productivity Powerhouse → Job descriptions → organized checklists → Messy resumes → perfect formatting → Email chaos → actionable task lists → Portfolio creation at scale

The difference?
❌ Using ChatGPT for everything = generic results 
✅ Using the right tool for each task = competitive advantage

Most candidates are still manually doing what these tools can automate in seconds.
While they're spending hours on one application, you're customizing 10.

--- Post 55 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Jan 14, 2026
Engagement: 245 reactions, 28 comments (Score: 1,505)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7409257745830379520

This international student came to the USA in 2024, in an absolutely insane job market, and within just 12 months of landing, she has already done two co-ops and is currently a Quality Engineering Intern at Tesla.

This is Riya Singh's Story. Just like most international students, when she landed in the US, there was no neat roadmap or "perfect" strategy.

At first, she: 
– Applied to everything that looked remotely relevant.
– Made her resume after coming to the US.
– Sent it into a hundred portals.

She realised very quickly that "spray and pray" was not going to work here. Things started to shift in the Spring. She met Maya Mishra on campus, now a PM at Walmart and ex-Tesla intern, and learned a lot. 

From there, Riya rebuilt her approach from the ground up:

– Applied early, often within the first 24 hours of a role going live
– Tweaked her resume for each posting instead of reusing the same version
–Checked sponsorship policies for internships instead of hoping it would "work out" later
– Asked, "Am I actually a good fit for this JD or just forcing it?"
– Stopped applying to roles that were a total mismatch, like heavy software positions that did not align with her background

On top of that, she leaned on her circle. Even friends who were interns. She built a solid rapport on campus, so when roles opened up, people were willing to attach their name to hers.

The Result? She got Multiple offers.

And right now, she is doing her Co-Op at a Tesla manufacturing plant, learning quality engineering in real time and stretching far beyond her original IT comfort zone.

A huge part of this story is also where she chose to study.

During her undergrad in India, she had already taken on leadership in clubs, teams, and events. She knew she wanted that mix of engineering plus management.

She chose Purdue University Master of Engineering Management Program (MEM) because:

– The coursework is flexible. She could pick subjects that match both her technical base and her leadership goals
–Tuition made sense. Brand Name and Affordable for her fees bracket. 
– Purdue is part of the "MEM Consortium" alongside some of the biggest universities in the world, which opens doors and networks that last beyond graduation
– The one-year cohort model, with the option to start co-ops after the first year, was perfect for someone who wanted industry exposure quickly

I still remember Eric VandeVoorde telling me about the MEM Consortium and how Purdue positions students to move between engineering and management roles. 

Riya’s journey is a reminder that:
– You can arrive in the "worst" market and still create your own opportunities
– People are often your most valuable resource if you build genuine relationships
– Internships are not lottery tickets; they are the result of aligned skills, timing, and strategy

Watch the full podcast: https://lnkd.in/gw5AQmVM

Take a look at what Purdue MEM Program offers → https://lnkd.in/g634EM_R

All Product Managers are the same?

If you think that… you’re wrong.

Product Management isn’t one-size-fits-all - each role comes with its own focus, skills, and impact.

Some PMs push growth with data, others shape long-term vision, while many balance tech, design, or operations. Knowing these differences helps you discover where you fit best or what kind of PM your company really needs.

Here are 10 Types of Product Managers you’ll find in the industry 👇

1. Technical PM – Aligns technical complexity with business goals for scalable, reliable solutions.

2. Growth PM – Runs experiments and data strategies to scale acquisition, engagement, and retention.

3. Platform PM – Builds APIs and platforms that empower internal teams and developers.

4. Consumer PM – Focuses on usability and delight to deliver intuitive, user-friendly products.

5. Enterprise PM – Manages client requirements, integrations, and scalability for B2B success.

6. Data PM – Turns pipelines into insights and powers smarter decisions.

7. AI/ML PM – Translates AI research into ethical, real-world applications.

8. Hardware PM – Oversees IoT, devices, and hardware-software integration.

9. Design/UX PM – Advocates for seamless, accessible user experiences.

10. Operations PM – Streamlines processes to deliver products efficiently.

Different PMs, different strengths - but all play a critical role in shaping products that matter.

P.S. Follow Pritesh Jagani for content like project managent and getting a job in tech field. 

Repost to help your network.

Author: priteshjagani
Date: Dec 15, 2025

Media: document
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7397761391408893952

Most job seekers use ChatGPT incorrectly....
But the top 1% use frameworks that turn AI into a career advantage.

If you want ChatGPT to write stronger resumes, better interview answers, and powerful career stories…
you need to stop giving vague prompts and start using proven structures.

Here are the Top 15 ChatGPT Frameworks every job seeker must know: 

1. STAR - Situation, Task, Action, Result
Turn messy experiences into clear, impact-driven stories that recruiters love.

2. CAR - Challenge, Action, Result
Highlight how you overcame obstacles and delivered meaningful results.

3. PAR - Problem, Action, Result
Show your problem-solving ability in a simple, powerful way.

4. CFR - Context, Fit, Results
Write personalised cover letters that explain why you are the perfect fit.

5. ATS-FIT - Keywords, Title, Skills, Fit
Optimize your resume using job-specific keywords and tailored alignment.

6. MAP - Match, Adapt, Personalize
Adjust your experience to match the company’s language and expectations.

7. MIRROR - Match Intent, Reflect Role, Optimize Resume
Rewrite your resume to mirror employer expectations with clarity.

8. KDA - Keywords, Duties, Accomplishments
Convert basic duties into measurable, achievement-focused bullet points.

9. SCAN - Skills, Competencies, Achievements, Needs
Craft strong professional summaries that match employer priorities.

10. CLEAR - Context, Leadership, Example, Achievement, Result
Showcase leadership experiences with structure and credibility.

11. PEAR - Project, Execution, Achievement, Relevance
Rewrite projects to highlight execution and job-aligned results.

12. ACE - Action, Contribution, Evidence
Demonstrate impact with clear actions and quantifiable proof.

13. STEP - Situation, Task, Execution, Proof
Turn raw experience into polished, value-driven achievements.

14. BRAG - Break Down Role, Responsibility, Achievement, Gain
Explain your role clearly while emphasizing standout accomplishments.

15. SMART - Specific, Measurable, Achievable, Relevant, Time-bound
Rewrite your goals or achievements with clarity and measurable depth.

ChatGPT becomes 10× more powerful when you give it structured frameworks,
because frameworks turn your experience into a story, and stories get you hired.

1. STAR - Situation, Task, Action, Result
Turn messy experiences into clear, impact-driven stories that recruiters love.

2. CAR - Challenge, Action, Result
Highlight how you overcame obstacles and delivered meaningful results.

3. PAR - Problem, Action, Result
Show your problem-solving ability in a simple, powerful way.

4. CFR - Context, Fit, Results
Write personalised cover letters that explain why you are the perfect fit.

5. ATS-FIT - Keywords, Title, Skills, Fit
Optimize your resume using job-specific keywords and tailored alignment.

6. MAP - Match, Adapt, Personalize
Adjust your experience to match the company’s language and expectations.

7. MIRROR - Match Intent, Reflect Role, Optimize Resume
Rewrite your resume to mirror employer expectations with clarity.

8. KDA - Keywords, Duties, Accomplishments
Convert basic duties into measurable, achievement-focused bullet points.

9. SCAN - Skills, Competencies, Achievements, Needs
Craft strong professional summaries that match employer priorities.

10. CLEAR - Context, Leadership, Example, Achievement, Result
Showcase leadership experiences with structure and credibility.

11. PEAR - Project, Execution, Achievement, Relevance
Rewrite projects to highlight execution and job-aligned results.

12. ACE - Action, Contribution, Evidence
Demonstrate impact with clear actions and quantifiable proof.

13. STEP - Situation, Task, Execution, Proof
Turn raw experience into polished, value-driven achievements.

14. BRAG - Break Down Role, Responsibility, Achievement, Gain
Explain your role clearly while emphasizing standout accomplishments.

15. SMART - Specific, Measurable, Achievable, Relevant, Time-bound
Rewrite your goals or achievements with clarity and measurable depth.

ChatGPT becomes 10× more powerful when you give it structured frameworks,
because frameworks turn your experience into a story, and stories get you hired.

This international student came to the US in 2024, in one of the toughest markets for jobseekers.

Within her first year at Purdue University Master of Engineering Management Program (MEM), she had already interviewed with and landed offers from companies like Tesla, Walmart and Zipline.

This is Venonshya Sunder’s story.

At first, she
– Applied to anything that looked even slightly relevant
– Put her resume together only after arriving on campus
– Uploaded it to portal after portal and hoped something would click

Very quickly, she saw that “spray and pray” was not a good strategy here. Things started to change when she rebuilt her approach from the inside out.

➤ 5–10 targeted applications a day, tracked and reviewed, even on heavy class days
➤ She rewrote her resume to sound like what she actually did & added more ownership, outcomes, and impact in numbers
➤ She stopped forcing bad fits.  Asked, “Is this role really aligned with my skills and direction, or am I just desperate to apply?” Cut out roles that were clearly mismatched instead of chasing everything

On top of that, she also leaned hard on her people.
– Reached out to seniors and alumni for honest feedback on her profile
– Used LinkedIn to connect with TPMs and engineers, not to beg for referrals but to understand what these roles actually look like
– Built a circle of classmates and roommates who shared openings, pushed each other to apply, and did not operate from scarcity

The result. Within months, she was converting offers. So far she has worked at Walmart, Tesla and is currently at Zipline.

A big part of this also ties back to where she chose to study.

She picked Purdue’s MEM because
– It sits at the intersection of engineering and management, which is exactly where she wanted to be
– The coursework let her stay technical while building leadership and business skills
– The ecosystem (career team, alumni, professional development funds, industry events) made it easier to get a foot in the door

Venonshya’s journey is a reminder that
– You can arrive in a “bad” market and still create momentum if your effort is focused
– Your past engineering work becomes powerful once you frame it as ownership and collaboration
– Internships and offers are rarely luck alone; they come from skills, timing, and the courage to keep going even when everyone around you is stressed and scared

Author: priteshjagani
Date: Nov 15, 2025

Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7385343563691319296

Everyone tells you to “network” to get a job.
But no one really explains how to reach out in a way that actually gets replies.

That’s where Cold Emailing comes in - It's not spam, not mass messages, but strategic communication with the right person, at the right time, with the right intent.

--> Find the right person.
Most people fail because they reach out to the wrong people. Instead of targeting CEOs or recruiters who rarely respond, find 10–15 professionals directly connected to the role you want - team leads, senior engineers, or heads of departments.

--> Define your “why” clearly.
Before you even start writing, get clear on your reason for reaching out. Avoid filler lines like “I wanted to connect.” Be specific - maybe you admire their work on a project, have feedback to share, or want their advice. 

--> Start with a small ask.
Don’t open with a big favor or a job request. Instead, ask for a short 10-minute chat or quick feedback. Small, respectful asks show confidence and make it easier for the other person to respond.

--> Personalize your message.
Spend a few minutes studying their work - read their posts, browse their GitHub, or check their talks. Mention something specific that stood out to you. That one line of personalization can make your email stand out.

--> Write a strong subject line.
Your subject decides whether your email gets opened. Keep it short, under 50 characters, and spark curiosity or value. Try lines like “Quick idea for your latest project” or “Loved your post on sustainable design.”

--> Keep it short and easy to read.
Busy professionals don’t have time for long intros. A simple format works best: who you are, why you’re reaching out, what’s in it for them, and a clear next step. Keep it under 120 words.

--> Follow up smartly.
Most replies don’t come from the first email. Wait 3–4 days, then send a polite follow-up. Keep it friendly and focused on value. Persistence pays off - many success stories come from the second or third message.

--> Track and optimize your outreach.
Use tools like Lemlist, Instantlyai, or Mailshake to track opens, replies, and clicks. Learn what works, adjust what doesn’t, and make every email better than the last.

Personalization always beats automation. Use simple, natural language, and give value before you ask for anything. A/B test your subject lines, and sign off with a clear, professional signature.

If you want to make your process smoother :
- Hunterio helps find verified emails.
- Instantlyai automates campaigns.
- ChatGPT / Claude helps you draft and personalize faster.

Cold emailing is about intentionality, clarity, and genuine curiosity.

Do it right, and one message could change your career.

Resume vs CV 
What’s the Real Difference? 

If you’ve ever used “resume” and “CV” interchangeably, you’re not alone. 
But here is the truth, they are not the same. And knowing the difference can make or break your next application.

Your Resume is a snapshot : short, targeted, and tailored for a specific job.
Your CV is a story : detailed, academic, and designed to showcase your full professional journey.

Here is the quick breakdown -

Resume: 
- 1–2 pages 
- Focused on relevant skills, impact, and achievements.
- Constantly updated and tailored per role.

CV: 
- 2–4+ pages
- Covers everything — education, research, awards, publications.
- Stays static, just refreshed with new experiences.

If you are applying for corporate roles, go with a resume.
If it is research or academia, you’ll need a CV.

Understand the difference. Build both versions.
Because the right document does not just list your experience, it lands you opportunities.

Here are the Top 15 trending technical careers in 2025 :

1. AI/ML Engineer – Building the brains behind intelligent systems.
2. Data Scientist / Data Engineer – Turning raw data into actionable insights.
3. Cybersecurity Specialist – Defending digital frontiers.
4. Cloud Architect / Cloud Engineer – Powering businesses on the cloud.
5. DevOps / Site Reliability Engineer – Ensuring seamless deployment & reliability.
6. Software Developer (Full-Stack) – Driving end-to-end product development.
7. Product Manager (Technical) – Bridging business and engineering.
8. IoT / Edge Computing Engineer – Making devices smarter & faster.
9. Blockchain / FinTech Engineer – Shaping the future of digital transactions.
10. AI Ethics / Responsible AI Specialist – Ensuring fairness & accountability in AI.
11. AR/VR Developer – Building immersive virtual worlds.
12. Robotics Engineer – Designing the future of automation.
13. Quantum Computing Specialist – Unlocking next-gen computational power.
14. UX/UI Designer (Tech Focused) – Creating user-first digital experiences.
15. Big Data Analyst – Transforming massive datasets into strategy.

The future belongs to those who adapt quickly. Whether you’re a student, job seeker, or tech professional - these are the roles to watch.

A few years ago, you could apply to 10–15 roles and expect interviews.

Today?
A single corporate job posting in the U.S. receives 200–300 applications.
Only 4–6 candidates typically get interviewed.

That’s less than a 3% chance if you apply blindly.

So what works now?

• Make your résumé hyper-relevant to the role
• Stop mass applying
• Start networking intentionally
• Talk about your projects, expertise, and impact
• Build real conversations, not just submissions

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7419426582026612736

I’ve interacted with 100s of students come to the US.
Some are geniuses who struggle. 
Some are average students who fly.

The difference? The ones who win realize early on that their 'technical skills' are only 50% of the equation. The other 50% is character.

I don’t mean to preach here. 
I say this in a very practical, career-changing way.

Because in the US, your progress rarely happens alone.

Yes, you do the right things.
Yes, you work hard.
Yes, you grind.

But your outcomes still depend on people:

– A senior who refers you.
– A classmate who sends you a lead.
– A roommate who helps you prep.
– A professor who gives you a lab role.
– A recruiter who replies after your second follow-up.
– A manager who gives you a chance when your resume is not perfect.

This is where character becomes a good differentiator. 

When you choose to be kind and helpful, you get asymmetrical returns.

↣ You help someone with an assignment once.
Later they pull you into a project that becomes your strongest resume story.

↣ You review a friend’s resume.
Later they connect you to their hiring manager.

↣ You share a resource without expecting anything back.
Months later, you are the first person they think of when an opportunity opens.

This is not luck.
It is reputation.

And reputation travels fast in universities, labs, student communities, and even inside companies.

Also, character is what protects you in the hard phases.

When you get rejected 50 times.
When you feel behind.
When the visa clock is loud.

If you are the person who stays steady, stays respectful, stays helpful, and keeps showing up, people root for you. And when people root for you, doors open that pure merit cannot open.

One more thing.

Being kind does not mean being naive.

It means:

↣ Helping without keeping score.
↣ Staying professional even when others are not.
↣ Not burning bridges when you are stressed.
↣ Being reliable when it is inconvenient.
↣ Giving credit where it is due. 
↣ Choosing integrity when nobody is watching.

If you want one unfair advantage as an international student, pick this one.

Build skills, yes.
Prepare for interviews, yes.

But do not ignore the thing that multiplies all of it.
Your character.

Because one day you will say, “I made it here.”

And if you are honest, you will also say:
“It was not my effort alone.”

============================================================

Gone are the days of blindly sending out hundreds of applications and hoping for a reply. 

Today, AI tools act as your personal career assistant - helping you discover the right jobs, optimize your resume, prepare for interviews, and even negotiate salaries. Instead of working harder, you can now search smarter, faster, and with more confidence.

1. Job Matching
Platforms like LinkedIn, ZipRecruiter, Eightfoldai, and Pymetrics analyze your skills and preferences to match you with the right opportunities.

2. Resume Optimization
Tools such as Rezi, Skillroads, Teal, Jobscan, and Resumakerai make sure your resume fits ATS requirements and highlights your strengths.

3. Salary Insights
With Salary.com, Payscale, and Glassdoor, you can negotiate with confidence by accessing real-time pay comparisons.

4. Search Assistants
AI bots like Google for Jobs, JobPal, Olivia by Paradox, JobWizard, and Teal streamline applications, scheduling, and job tracking.

5. Interview Prep
Platforms including InterviewBuddy, ChatGPT (OpenAI), and Final Round AI provide mock interviews, feedback, and practice to boost confidence.

AI is changing the way we apply, prepare, and succeed. Job seekers who use these tools can stand out.

You didn't get the offer.

Again.
And you're starting to wonder if it's your resume.
Your experience.
Your degree.

It's not.

Most candidates have the hard skills.
They can code. They can analyze. They can execute.

But here's what hiring managers actually look for:
- Can you communicate clearly in a meeting?
- Can you handle conflict without burning bridges?
- Can you adapt when priorities shift overnight?

These are the skills that separate the interview from the offer.

Technical skills get you in the room.
Soft skills get you the job.

Adaptability. Communication. Problem-solving.
Leadership. Emotional intelligence. Time management.

These aren't buzzwords.
They're what make you hireable.
And promotable.

Most people ignore them.
Focus on certifications. Degrees. Tools.

But when two candidates have similar experience,
the one who can work well with people wins.

Every. Single. Time.

So ask yourself:
Are you just building your resume? Or are you building yourself?

Because in 2025-26, soft skills aren't optional.
They're the difference between applying and landing.

--- Post 84 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Feb 13, 2026
Engagement: 227 reactions, 5 comments, 1 reposts (Score: 1,187)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7427769450914938880

Great Job Hunting Strategy for International Students:

Btw, I run a community of 5100+ jobseekers, my Job Hunting Accelerator, where I regularly share a lot of battle-tested advice I see working out in real time with international students and jobseekers. 

We've got modules for networking, job hunting strategy, and have tons of guest sessions as well. No pressure to join, if you feel you want more support in your journey, check it out here: https://lnkd.in/gt5YBAbr

1. Build a sharp resume. Don’t make it fancy or crowded. One page that shows skills, projects, and outcomes the way a recruiter reads.

2. Learn how to pitch yourself in 30 seconds. Clear role, clear stack, clear strengths.

3. Apply with structure. Track every role, follow up every week, and refine based on data. Random applications create random results.

4. Build a portfolio, follow this flow. Real problems  ➡︎ clean documentation  ➡︎ measurable impact. Tutorial apps don’t survive recruiter screens.

5. Understand the market. Which companies sponsor? Which roles prefer fresh grads? Which hiring cycles matter? Blind effort wastes time.

6. Master behavioral interviews. Every answer should show clarity, ownership, and results. Underestimating these is a big blunder many make. 

7. Build a network before you need it. Alumni, seniors, campus clubs, professors. Warm intros cut rejection rates by half.

8. Know your visa timeline because your life depends on it. CPT, OPT, STEM OPT. Apply early, not when your clock is about to expire.

9. Practice technical rounds under stress. Timed OAs, mock interviews, and pattern recognition. Skill matters, but composure decides the outcome.

10. Keep your momentum when others get offers before you. 

This is the goal.
You may not have all of these yet.

But move in this direction.

The difference between someone who gets a job in the US and someone who gets pushed out is not talent alone.

It is discipline.
It is clarity.
It is a systems approach to the job search.

Build that.

============================================================

--- Post 85 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Dec 15, 2025

Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7398836324692262913

If you’re preparing for job interviews in 2025, ChatGPT + a few smart AI tools can give you a huge advantage.

Here's a guide that breaks down the exact prompts you can use to prepare for any interview - from domain knowledge and behavioral questions to technical deep dives and resume-based prep.

Here’s are all the ChatGPT Prompts listed:

📌 Domain-Specific Prep
Prompt:
"I am preparing for an interview in the [Industry/Domain] (e.g., FinTech, Healthcare, EdTech). What are domain-specific questions and trends I should be aware of?"

📌 Resume-Based Interview Questions
Prompt:
"Based on my resume below, generate 10 likely interview questions and the best possible answers. My resume: [Paste your resume]"

📌 Role-Specific Technical Interview Prep
Prompt:
"I'm applying for a [Job Role] role at [Company Name]. Provide me with technical interview questions and sample answers based on the required skills: [List skills like Python, SQL, etc.]"

📌 Behavioral Interview Mastery
Prompt:
"Give me 10 common behavioral interview questions for [Job Role], and help me answer them using the STAR method. I have experience in [Your Experience/Projects]."

📌 Tailored Interview Question Generator
Prompt:
"Based on this job description: [Paste Job Description], what are the top 15 questions I might be asked in an interview? Include ideal answers based on industry best practices."

📌 Technical Deep Dive Prompts
Prompt:
"Explain [Topic] in simple terms for an interview. Then ask me 3 follow-up questions and answers an interviewer might ask to test deep understanding."
Example topics:
• How REST APIs work
• Big-O Notation
• Kubernetes
• Architecture
• ETL Pipelines

📌 Portfolio/Project Questions
Prompt:
"Here is a project I worked on: [Describe your project]. What questions might I be asked about this in a technical interview? Help me frame confident and clear answers."

📌 Mock Interview Roleplay Prompt
Prompt:
"Act like a hiring manager for a [Job Role] position at [Company Name]. Ask me 5 rounds of interview questions. After each of my answers, give me feedback and suggest improvements."

With the right prompts and supporting AI tools, interview prep becomes faster, clearer, and far more effective - helping you walk into every interview confident and ready.

And if you want to turn recruiter visibility into real job offers, that’s exactly what we teach inside the Job Hunting Accelerator, where Big Tech recruiters train international students to win in today’s market: https://lnkd.in/gxt5X9BC
