My advice on landing jobs in the USA after spending 8+ years in the market and coaching 4500+ job seekers:

[1] Start before you land, not after you panic

Most students say, “I will start once I reach the US.”
By the time they land, reality hits. 
New culture. New system. New pressure.
Job hunting becomes reactive instead of strategic.

The ones who win start months earlier.
Resume ready. LinkedIn ready. Portfolio ready.

I have seen students apply in June and July while landing in August and crack internships within three months.

Zero US experience. Still hired.
All because they started early.

Early preparation is not motivation.
It is positioning.

Btw, Every week, I share insights like this in my job hunting accelerator with over 4100+ people. If you want to get hired in the next 3 months, join my academy: https://lnkd.in/gWWZJDEf

We’ve got modules on everything:
- Networking, resume, job strategy
- focus sessions with the community
- live sessions with Hiring Managers
- guest sessions with recruiters and immigration lawyers

With 100s of stories to back it all up. 

[2] Build relationships that vouch for you

Most people think networking means cold messages and asking for referrals.
That is not networking. 

Good networking is when someone inside a company walks up to their hiring manager and says,
“I know this person. You should interview them.”

That level of trust is not built in one message.
It is built through time, value, and consistency.

People get jobs from conferences.
– From hackathons.
– From Slack groups.
– From birthday parties. (yes, even this!)

From random conversations where they showed up as humans, not job seekers.

Your mindset has to shift from “I need a referral” to “I want to build real relationships.”

In the US, your network eventually becomes your net worth.
Whether you like it or not.

[3] Use location as leverage, even if your university is small

A good city for job seekers is not about vibes.
It is about access.

– Access to meetups.
– Access to conferences.
– Access to people who have already done what you want to do.

And if your college is in a small town, that is not a death sentence.
It just means you travel for opportunities instead of waiting for them.

I have seen students from tiny college towns go to major hackathons in San Francisco, meet engineers from big tech, and walk away with interviews and offers.

One student went to a major hackathon with no guarantees.
Today he works at Amazon.

Exposure made all the difference.

–

--- Post 247 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Feb 13, 2026
Engagement: 34 reactions, 5 comments (Score: 220)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7422683722346455040

7 things I would tell you about building a great resume for job hunting in 2026 if I weren't afraid of hurting your feelings.

Btw, I run a community of 4800+ jobseekers, my Job Hunting Accelerator, where I've regularly given this advice to so many international students.

We've got modules for networking, job hunting strategy, and have tons of guest sessions as well. Check it out here: https://lnkd.in/gt5YBAbr

Listen closely to this.

[1] Your resume is invisible, not bad

Most people are writing for humans while the first “reader” is a robot. If the language on your resume does not look like the job description, you get filtered out before a recruiter ever learns your name.

↣ For every job, highlight the core keywords in the JD and check if they exist in your resume in the same or very similar wording.

↣ Stop trusting your gut, use Ctrl+F on both the JD and your resume and match the language deliberately.

[2] Your “beautiful” Canva template is killing your chances

Two columns, icons, timelines and fancy layouts might look good on Instagram, but many ATS parsers still scramble them. If the software cannot read it cleanly, it is as good as not applying.

↣ Use a simple single-column layout with standard section headings like “Experience”, “Education”, “Skills”.

↣ No tables, no images, no icons, no text boxes, no resumes exported as flat images or from strange formats.

↣ Save as a normal text-based PDF or Word file and test it by copy pasting everything into a blank document to see if it comes out in the right order.

[3] You are listing tasks instead of providing outcomes

“Managed a team” or “Responsible for X” is the language of people who do not get callbacks. People doing the screening care about whether you moved numbers, not whether you attended meetings.

↣ Rewrite every bullet to be action plus metric, for example “Led a team of 5 to increase quarterly revenue by 15 percent” instead of “Managed a team”.

↣ If you do not know the exact number, use reasonable ranges or percentages that are honest. Even “Reduced ticket backlog from 120 to under 40 in two months” is fine.

↣ Put your strongest and most measurable bullets at the top of each role, not hidden in the middle.

[4] You are trying to use one resume for every role
The “shotgun” approach of one generic resume for 50 jobs is dead. The people who are getting offers are sending fewer applications with sharper tailoring.

↣ Create two or three base resumes for your main target role types, for example “Backend Engineer”, “Full stack Engineer”, “Data Engineer”.

↣ Before each application, adapt only the top part summary, the job title, and the most relevant bullets so they mirror that specific JD.

↣ Keep a folder where you save each tailored version with the company and role in the file name so you can reuse and track what you sent.

Continued ↓

Author: priteshjagani

Engagement: 40 reactions, 1 comments (Score: 210)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7425229364549558272

5 legal side hustles for folks on H1B visas that can generate anywhere from $1,000 to $10,000 monthly, depending on your strengths. 

If you are on an H1B, you already know the golden rule: you cannot “work” for anyone except the employer who sponsors your visa.

So anything you do on the side has to be truly passive, no services, no active operations. 

Quick note: this is for education only, not legal, tax, or immigration advice. Always double-check with an attorney or CPA who understands H1B rules before you move money.

Also, apart from this post, I've also made a detailed guide on passive income on H1-B Visa, comment Guide and I'll send the link over. 

[1] Real estate, without becoming a landlord
You are allowed to own assets, but you cannot actively run a real estate business.

Ways to do it

- REITs
Think of them like mutual funds for big commercial properties. You buy shares, the company owns hospitals, malls, apartments, and sends you dividends every month or quarter. Example, “monthly dividend” REITs that yield roughly 5 percent.

- Crowdfunded real estate
Platforms pool money from many small investors into large projects. Minimums can start as low as a few hundred dollars. You do not deal with tenants or contractors; you just buy units and track returns.

- Rental property with a property manager
If you want to own a house or condo, you must pay a professional manager to handle showings, tenants, repairs, and listing. They usually charge 5 to 10 percent of rent. You build equity, get rent, and may get tax benefits from depreciation, without lifting a hammer.

[2] Stock market investing

This is the cleanest side hustle for H1B holders, since you are not performing a job, you are just putting your money into public markets.

How people use it

- Broad index funds like S and P 500 for long term growth
- Dividend stocks and ETFs that pay 3 to 5 percent yearly and can be reinvested
- Automatic monthly investing through brokers like Fidelity, Vanguard, etc

No extra paperwork for the visa, but you still need to understand risk, taxes, and your time horizon.

[3] Oil and gas projects
Big drilling projects need huge amounts of capital. Some companies let outside investors fund wells and share a slice of the revenue.

For H1B this works only if

- You are a passive investor in a structure like a Direct Participation Program
- You do not manage operations, staff, or decisions

Upside

- Potential returns in the high single digits to mid twenties in some cases
- Very strong tax benefits in some programs, where a large part of the investment can be written off in year one

Downside, it is a niche area with high risk, you must understand the operator and the contract before you say yes.

Continued ↓

--- Post 256 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Dec 15, 2025
Engagement: 32 reactions, 4 comments (Score: 200)
Media: video
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7405071416137613313

Shiva left America and went back home

When I spoke to him, he shared the reality of the U.S job market which is nothing like what you see on Instagram.

Here’s what he told me 

When most of us think of U.S. jobs, we think about shiny office, Great pay and Stability.

But what actually happens behind the scenes?

Big companies don’t hire everyone directly.

I mean most legit U.S. companies hire full-time employees normally.

But in some sectors, especially IT contracting, big companies give the work to another company called a prime vendor. 

And these prime vendors then reach out to dozens of Desi consultancies

Let’s say Facebook sets a pay rate of $100 an hour.
The prime vendor lists it as an $80 job.
Then the consultancy picks it up and says, “We’ll get you this job, but we’ll keep 20% of your paycheck… forever.”

your $80 now becomes around $64.

So, On paper, his salary is high… and when he files for H-1B, he’s taxed on the full amount and not the $64 he actually takes home.

Be fully prepared with strong skills, because that’s the only thing no one can take from you in this brutal job market

If you're in a smaller U.S. city… this might surprise you.

The U.S. government uses the OEWS wage system, meaning salary levels vary by location.

A salary that qualifies as Wage Level 4 in a smaller Ohio town might only be Level 1 or 2 in Silicon Valley.

And under the new proposed H-1B lottery structure, your number of entries depends on your wage level, giving some smaller-city employees a higher chance of selection.

This creates a fascinating shift:
Lower-cost cities may become more attractive for employers due to stronger wage-level positioning under the lottery system.

→ It’s knowing your real budget, and not blowing ₹50L on FOMO.

It shouldn’t be “Job-ready vs. Just survive.”
→ It’s networking with seniors and pros *before* you need help, not after you’re desperate.

It shouldn’t be “One perfect resume vs. 10 spammy templates.”
→ It’s building a profile that’s clear, credible, and *stands up* in a career fair.

It shouldn’t be “Should I DM 3 people or 30?”
→ It’s putting in the reps, outreach is a numbers game, not a vibes game.

It shouldn’t be “Should I apply to 100 jobs or wait for referrals?”
→ It’s both early and often. No one owes you a shortcut.

It shouldn’t be “Do I need a scholarship vs. do I need a loan?”
→ It’s being brutally honest about money from day 1, not after the admit email.

It shouldn’t be “On-campus job vs. focus on GPA.”
→ It’s doing both because you’ll need survival cash AND a strong transcript.

It shouldn’t be “Conferences vs. chilling with friends.”
→ It’s choosing what gets you closer to the offer letter, not just what feels comfortable.

Stop debating what doesn’t matter.
Start doing the work that actually moves you forward.

and outworking the 99% who just talk about it.

--- Post 274 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Feb 13, 2026
Engagement: 31 reactions, 1 comments, 1 reposts (Score: 167)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7422790670798630912

You have been in the U.S. for 8 years. You fly home for a 2-week wedding, and as you reach the airport, you get an email: “Your appointment is cancelled for social media vetting. Your new date is May 2027.”

Just like that, your job, your apartment, your car, and your daily life in the U.S. are all gone because you forgot to list a Reddit handle you have not used since college.

In 2026, this kind of disruption is no longer a movie script. It is a very real possibility.

Every Wednesday, I sit with an immigration lawyer on my YouTube channel. This post is based on my recent conversation with Manifest Law about social media vetting, DS 160 forms, and visa stamping.

Here is what you need to understand.

1. Social media is now part of your file

- Visa appointments getting rescheduled for “administrative processing” and security checks
- Social media vetting being one of the reasons cases are held
- Some people being pushed many months, even years, into the future

You are no longer just a passport and a petition.
You are your DS 160 answers plus your online footprint.

2. The DS 160 social media question is not optional in practice

If you:

- List some platforms but “forget” others
- Skip old accounts because “I never use them”
- Assume no one will ever find your anonymous profile

You are taking a risk you may not even see.

From the call:

- If you missed a handle with no activity, it can usually be explained at the interview
- The bigger issue is when there are clear mismatches, hidden accounts, or content that contradicts what you declared
- Social media checks can trigger more scrutiny and longer timelines, even if you eventually get approved

3. Before you travel for stamping, do this

If you are on H1B, F1 OPT, STEM, or any status that needs visa stamping soon, treat this like part of your documentation:

- Make a list of every social platform where you have an account

+ Instagram
+ Facebook
+ Twitter
+ LinkedIn
+ Reddit
+ TikTok
+ Any older platform you used with your real identity
+ Match this list against what you will enter in DS 160
+ Be honest about inactive accounts too, even if you have not logged in for years

If you already submitted DS 160 and forgot a handle:

+ Do not panic
+ Take a printed list of all your handles to the interview
+ Be ready to explain calmly that it was an oversight, especially if there is no real activity there

Disclaimer: I am not an attorney. This post is for educational purposes only and is based on my public conversation with an immigration lawyer. Your case may be very different. Before you make decisions about travel, stamping or DS 160 answers, speak with a qualified immigration professional who can review your specific situation and documents.

--

--- Post 282 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Mar 15, 2026
Engagement: 31 reactions (Score: 155)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7435017593649790976

"Between the filing date and the final action date in the Visa Bulletin, which one actually gets me a green card?"

This is one of the most common questions we get on the livestream. Let me break down what each date means.

Every Wednesday, I host an immigration Q&A with Manifest Law on my YouTube channel. 

If you're thinking of applying for O-1, EB-1, and other talent-based visas, grab a 30-min free consultation & see if you qualify: https://lnkd.in/gbb3aTpa

➤ Filing Date (Chart A):

This is the day you can apply for an adjustment of status.
When your priority date becomes current for filing, you can submit:

- I-485 (adjustment of status application)
- I-765 (work permit/EAD application)
- I-131 (travel document/advance parole application)

You file all three together based on your approved I-140.

Once you file, you can get work authorization and travel authorization while you wait for your green card to be approved.

This is the date USCIS uses to determine if you can start the process and maintain authorized stay in the United States.

➤ Final Action Date (Chart B):

This is when your green card actually gets approved.

This is the date when USCIS will process your I-485 and either:

- Approve your green card and mail it to you, or
- Call you for an interview right before approval

➤ The Gap Between Them:

The final action date is usually behind the filing date. Even when filing dates are current for Rest of World countries, the final action date might still be months or years behind because it takes time to process the actual adjustment of status.

It used to take 6-9 months between filing and approval. Now it's taking much longer, especially for Indian nationals because of the waiting times.

➤ Which One Matters More?

The filing date is actually the more important one to watch.

Why? Because that's the date that lets you:
- Apply for your green card process
- Get your work permit so you're not dependent on H-1B/L-1
- Get travel authorization so you can leave and re-enter the US
- Maintain legal status while you wait

The final action date tells you when you'll actually get the green card, but the filing date is what unlocks the ability to apply and get those interim benefits.

➤ What This Means Practically:

If you see "Current" for filing dates in your category:

- You can file I-485 immediately if you have an approved I-140
- You'll get work and travel authorization within a few months of filing
- But your green card approval will depend on the final action date

If your final action date is still years behind:

- You've started the process and have protection
- You can work and travel freely
- But you'll wait for final approval based on that second date

Disclaimer: I am not an attorney. This is for educational purposes only based on conversations with immigration lawyers. Speak with a qualified immigration attorney about your specific priority date and timeline.

============================================================

H-1B Lottery 2026 — Important Dates & Major Change.

If you’re planning for this cycle, read this carefully:

📅 Registration Opens: March 7, 2026 (12 PM EST)
📅 Registration Closes: March 24, 2026 (12 PM EST)
🗓 Employers can create accounts starting February 28
💰 $215 non-refundable registration fee per beneficiary

Cap Structure:
• 65,000 Regular Cap
• 20,000 U.S. Master’s Cap

Selections are expected around March 31.

If selected, employers can file Form I-129 starting April 1.

Your USCIS account will show:
• Submitted
• Selected
• Not Selected

🚨 Biggest change this year: The lottery is wage-based.

Higher wage level = higher probability of selection.

That changes strategy significantly.

Author: priteshjagani

Media: video
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7435821522281209856

If the H-1B lottery becomes wage-based, your strategy matters more than ever.

Here are 4 things you should check immediately:

1️⃣ Identify the correct SOC code
Your SOC code is based on your job responsibilities, not just your job title.

2️⃣ Ensure your employer registers you on time
Registration must be completed through the USCIS portal before the March deadline.

3️⃣ Check your wage level
Your wage level is determined by your job role and location (zip code).
Higher wage level = higher probability in a wage-based system.

4️⃣ Discuss salary with your employer
In some cases, adjusting salary may qualify you for a higher wage level.
The salary requirement typically needs to take effect starting October 1.

These insights were discussed in our live session with an immigration lawyer from Manifest Law.

Watch this clip
And here is the link for the Wage calculator: https://lnkd.in/g7pPGtEU

--- Post 302 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Mar 15, 2026
Engagement: 23 reactions, 1 comments (Score: 125)
Media: image
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7437527790431657984

If you’re an Indian immigrant in the United States, do not send your money home using Wise or Remitly.

Most people use these platforms because they’re popular and easy, and I understand. However, a lot of these apps hide the fees inside the exchange rate. Google will show you $1 is around ₹90.

But when you actually transfer money:
– you’ll either pay a “transaction fee” (like $3.99), or
– they’ll quietly give you a worse conversion rate than the real one

Either way, you lose.

Example.
If you send $1,000 to your family, you expect roughly ₹90,800. But they end up getting something like ₹88,645.

That gap looks small on one transfer. Over time, you’re literally handing these apps thousands of dollars for absolutely nothing.

I found a better alternative: Crobo.money. It’s built to give you the true market rate, and sometimes even a better rate than what Google shows.

I’ve been using it myself for a year.

What I like:
- Zero transaction fees
- NO hidden charges
- Money delivered in seconds
- Trusted by 50,000+ users with 1M+ successful transactions

If anything goes wrong, they offer a 100% refund

As a member of my community, you'll receive a $25 Amazon gift card once you complete a transfer of $500 or more.

Here’s the link, check it out: https://lnkd.in/gXphVs2H

--- Post 303 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Mar 15, 2026
Engagement: 23 reactions, 1 comments (Score: 125)
Media: video
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7432928218208399360

There are a lot of myths about immigrants.

Let’s look at facts instead of assumptions.

Myth 1: Immigrants steal jobs and lower wages.
Reality: Immigrants don’t just work, they spend, build businesses, and create jobs.

Myth 2: America is easy to move to.
Reality: Some green card categories can take decades. Other developed countries are often easier.

Myth 3: Immigrants abuse welfare.
Reality: Immigrants use government healthcare 42% less than native-born citizens, and many benefits aren’t accessible for the first five years.

Myth 4: Immigrants increase national debt.
Reality: There’s no evidence of this. Immigrants pay taxes into systems they may never benefit from.

Myth 5: Immigrants ruin infrastructure.
Reality: Growing cities reflect economic activity. Growth brings tax revenue and development.

Whether immigrant or native-born, most people are simply trying to build a better life.

What’s a myth about immigrants you’ve heard that isn’t true?

============================================================

Planning to apply for a Green Card in 2026? Read this carefully.

It’s no longer just about filling out forms correctly.

There are 5 major areas getting stricter attention:

1️⃣ Employer History Checks
USCIS is reviewing mergers, layoffs, and whether the sponsoring company can still pay the offered wage.

2️⃣ Surprise Worksite Visits
Unannounced visits are increasing. Officers verify your role, location, and employer details.

3️⃣ Mandatory In-Person Interviews
Marriage-based cases are seeing fewer interview waivers. Both spouses must appear and answer questions face-to-face.

4️⃣ Digital Footprint Scrutiny
Social media and online presence are under greater review.

5️⃣ Public Charge Considerations
Even without taking benefits, factors like age, income, and health may be evaluated.

2026 filings will require more preparation, documentation, and strategy.

This was discussed in our live session with an immigration lawyer from Manifest Law.

--- Post 316 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Jan 14, 2026
Engagement: 21 reactions, 1 comments (Score: 115)

URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7406724863677169665

This one financial decision is quietly saving international students thousands of dollars, and most people still have no idea it even exists.

Take the example of Rahul, whom I met a while ago, and like most international students, he came to the U.S. with an education loan from India. 

At the beginning, the EMI felt manageable. 
He had a job offer in hand. The future felt predictable. 
But then reality slowly caught up.

Every month, he had to convert dollars to rupees. 
Exchange rates kept changing. Interest kept adding up. 

Even though his salary increased, the loan felt heavier with every passing year. 
It did not feel like progress. It felt like running in place.

For a long time, he did what most students do. He kept paying and hoping it would somehow become easier with time. It did not.

That is when he learned about refinancing.

In simple terms, refinancing means that a U.S.-based lender takes over your Indian education loan. From that point onward, your repayment happens inside the U.S. financial system instead of outside it. 

– You stop fighting currency conversion every month. 
– You stop being exposed to unpredictable forex swings. 
– You start dealing with interest rates and repayment structures that are actually designed for your current income reality.

For Rahul, the impact was immediate and practical.

His interest rate came down. 
The total amount he would pay over the life of the loan is reduced. 

At the same time, his repayments started contributing to his U.S. credit history, something most international students completely miss in their early working years.

A strong credit history later affects everything. 
– Your ability to rent easily.
– Even long-term planning tools like 401k accounts. 

Right now, there is only one U.S.-based lender that offers this refinancing structure specifically for international students in a reliable way. That lender is MPOWER Financing.

Rahul went through this process and is already seeing the difference not just in his monthly stress levels, but in how his financial future in the U.S. is shaping up.

It takes less than sixty seconds to check if you are eligible. Before sending your next EMI without thinking, it is worth knowing whether you can reduce both your interest burden and your long-term financial risk at the same time.

Link: https://lnkd.in/e2-pa7b9

Most students think financial growth only comes from earning more.
The smarter ones realize that it also comes from paying less for the same future.

--- Post 334 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Jan 14, 2026
Engagement: 20 reactions (Score: 100)
Media: video
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7406000023903199232

Work permit rules in America just changed and here's what you need to know

USCIS has officially reduced the validity period of many work permits (EADs)

Earlier, some categories got 5-year work permits. but Now Most will be cut back to 18 months or even 1 year.

Why? USCIS says this allows them to “vet people more often”
and prevent security risks.

Ok now if you’re in one of these categories, your EAD will now max out at 18 months and If your I-765 is pending or filed on/after Dec 5, 2025, this applies to you.

Under the new H.R.1 law that was passed in July 2025, certain categories that you see on screen will receive EADs valid for just one year or even shorter.

and for these people If their I-765 is pending or filed on/after July 22, 2025, this rule applies to them as well. 

Shorter EAD won’t affect your eligibility, but it will affect your renewal cycles, timelines, travel plans, and job security.

So, Track your EAD expiration date closely, Talk to an attorney, Apply early and Plan travel and job changes carefully.

By the way, I'm not an immigration lawyer, but I do have my immigration lawyer from Manifest , who I do my live sessions with, which will be happening every single Wednesday on my YouTube channel.

Five years ago, students mostly asked me about computer science or data or product.

This year? Robotics, IoT, and autonomous systems came up again and again.

We’ve all seen how fast these fields are growing. Autonomous vehicles, drones, smart factories, precision agriculture - industries are already investing heavily.

But here’s the gap: the demand for talent is high, while the supply of skilled professionals hasn’t caught up yet.

That’s exactly why Purdue University has launched a Purdue University Autonomy, Internet of Things (IoT), and Robotics Professional Master's Programs

I had an hour long conversation with Eric VandeVoorde. He explained that this program isn’t just another Master’s.

Here’s why it stood out to me:
1. The very first batch has only 36 students. That means small class sizes, real mentorship, and access to faculty.

2. It’s backed by 100+ professors from Purdue’s engineering departments - mechanical, electrical, aerospace, computer science, and more.

3. The program is project-heavy, not just theory. Students are already working on drones, autonomous driving, spacecraft systems, and robotics projects.

4. Companies are noticing. Even though it’s brand new, students from the first cohort already have co-ops and internships at Cummins, ZF, Siemens Energy, and Shark Ninja.

5. And on top of that, they get exposure to seminars with Amazon Robotics, Waymo, Toyota Research, Saab, Honda Research, and many more.

What I liked most from my conversation with Eric is that this program sits at the intersection of cutting-edge research and real industry application.

It’s not a traditional thesis program, but it’s not just coursework either - it’s applied, hands-on, and designed to prepare you for industry roles.

For anyone considering Fall 2026 or Spring 2026 intake, and you’re serious about robotics, IoT, or autonomous systems, this is a program you should look into.

Check out the Program details here: https://lnkd.in/gRpE3m2A

Every month, thousands of international graduates in the U.S. send money home to pay off Indian education loans, only to lose a significant portion to forex conversion, high interest rates, and hidden fees.

Let’s say you borrowed ₹40 lakhs (~$50,000) from India at 16% interest.
Now you’re earning in the U.S.

You transfer money every month, pay ₹68 - ₹70 per dollar, add transaction charges…

Your total monthly hit? Around $838.

But if you moved that same loan to a U.S.-based one at ~10% interest, your payment drops to $730/month.

That’s $108 saved every month.

Over 10 years, that’s $13,000 - $14,000 in savings.

And that’s before you count exchange-rate headaches disappearing.
That simple switch is called refinancing - converting your INR loan into USD once you start earning in the U.S.

Most students never hear about it.
- Their banks don’t tell them.
- Their agents don’t know it’s possible.

So they keep paying more than they should.

Here’s what changes when you refinance with MPOWER Financing (the only lender currently doing INR-to-USD refinancing for international students):

✅Release your collateral and co-signer, & simplify your payments.
✅ Build U.S. credit history through on-time payments as MPOWER reports this to the credit bureaus.
✅ Eligible for U.S. tax benefits on interest paid.

You don’t need to be a finance pro to get this right.

You just need to ask one question: “If I’m living and earning in the U.S., why am I still paying a loan in rupees?”

For most international graduates, refinancing isn’t optional anymore - it’s the simplest way to cut costs, build credit, and gain control.

If your Indian loan feels heavier now that you’re earning in the U.S., it’s time to run the numbers.

MPOWER built a product exactly for that situation.

You’ll also get $100 cashback at disbursement.

Most people think job hunting is just applying to jobs.

It's not.

It's a full-time job itself.

Here's what nobody tells you about the process:

You'll spend hours tailoring resumes.
You'll optimize your LinkedIn profile 10 times.
You'll network with strangers who might ghost you.
You'll write cover letters that no one reads.

And then the real fun starts:

You'll get ghosted after interviews.
You'll receive rejections with zero feedback.
You'll sit in silence wondering if they even saw your application.

It's brutal.

But here's what I learned after going through it:

The people who succeed don't have better resumes.

They have better systems.

They track every application.
They follow up consistently.
They ask for feedback even when it hurts.
They treat networking like a daily habit, not a desperate move.

And most importantly:

They don't take rejection personally.

Because job hunting isn't about being perfect.

It's about being persistent.

Every no gets you closer to the right yes.
Every rejection teaches you something.
Every ghost reminds you to keep moving forward.

The right opportunity is out there.

You just have to outlast the process.

Does a bonus count toward meeting the H-1B prevailing wage requirement?
This is a critical distinction many candidates misunderstand.

In most cases:
• Base salary is what counts toward the prevailing wage
• Bonuses, commissions, and variable pay generally do not count

For an H-1B petition to be compliant, the employer must guarantee at least the required wage level through base pay.

Every international graduate in the U.S. is paying a hidden tax.
And it’s not from the IRS.

It’s called a currency mismatch: earning in USD but repaying your education loan in INR.

It doesn’t look like a tax.

But check your monthly statement, and you’ll see it:
- Conversion fees
- High Indian interest rates
- The “forex dip” you can’t predict

Let’s do quick math.
₹40 lakhs = roughly $50,000.
At 16% interest, you’re paying $838/month for 10 years.
Every time the rupee weakens by ₹1, you pay $7- $10 more that month.

Now multiply that by 120 payments.

You didn’t sign up for it, but you’re paying for it.

That’s why many international students quietly refinance their Indian loans into U.S.-based loans once they start earning.

It’s not just a rate cut. It’s damage control.

When you refinance into USD:
 ✅ You eliminate conversion loss
 ✅ You build your U.S. credit history
 ✅ You can claim U.S. tax deductions on student loan interest
 ✅ You free your parents from being co-signers

And if you pick the right lender, there’s no prepayment penalty, either.

The only lender currently offering INR-to-USD refinancing for international students? 

MPOWER Financing: https://lnkd.in/e2-pa7b9

They’ve built this product for people exactly at this stage - earning, independent, but still paying old-currency loans.

If you want to think of it simply:
Refinancing isn’t an upgrade. It’s a correction.

You’ve already changed countries, jobs, and salaries.
Your loan deserves to catch up too.

Best Time to Apply for STEM OPT: Do NOT miss this window.

Get Free 30 Min Consultation and Profile Evaluation for EB-1A / NIW / talent-based visas : https://lnkd.in/gbb3aTpa

A lot of students wait too long and risk gaps in status.

Here’s what you need to remember:

• You can apply up to 90 days before your current EAD expires
• USCIS must receive your application by your EAD end date
• The “mailbox rule” does NOT apply
• You must file within 60 days of the STEM OPT I-20 recommendation

If you miss the 60-day window you cannot extend, even if your EAD hasn’t expired yet.

In this clip, we break down with immigration lawyer from Manifest Law the timing strategy so you don’t put your status at risk.

--- Post 427 ---
Author: priteshjagani
Date: Mar 15, 2026
Engagement: 10 reactions (Score: 50)
Media: video
URL: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7433715144062459905

How to expedite biometrics after an OPT RFE?

Many students panic when they receive a biometrics notice after an OPT Request for Evidence.

Here’s what you should know:

• Biometrics is scheduled by USCIS — you cannot simply “skip” it
• You may request rescheduling if there’s a genuine conflict
• Expedite requests are only granted under specific criteria (severe financial loss, humanitarian reasons, etc.)
• Walking into ASC early is generally not allowed unless specifically permitted

The most important step: respond to the RFE accurately and completely before worrying about speed.

This was discussed in our live session with an immigration lawyer from Manifest Law.

Watch this clip
and to watch full live session here is the link : https://lnkd.in/evBFnXRf

Does your SOC code impact H-1B selection?
Not directly — but it plays a critical role.

Here’s why:
• SOC code determines the prevailing wage range
• That range determines your wage level
• Wage level determines how many chances you get in selection

Under the new wage-based H-1B system, choosing the correct and defensible SOC code is more important than ever.

We covered this in detail with Manifest Law to avoid misinformation and risky assumptions.

Does Premium Processing mean you won’t get biometrics?

This is a very common misconception and the short answer is no.

Premium Processing only speeds up how fast USCIS reviews your case.
It does not automatically waive biometrics if USCIS decides they’re required.

In this clip, we explain:
• What Premium Processing actually does
• Why biometrics may still be requested
• When biometrics are reused vs re-scheduled
• What to expect if you get a biometrics notice
